### Follow-up scope: paired snapshot connection ownership

The read-only scan found a concrete raw SQLite handle leak in `app/snapshot.py:create_snapshot`: in paired mode, the source connection is acquired before the destination, but the cleanup block starts only after both connections succeed. If destination acquisition raises, the paired snapshot error path cleans output files but leaves the source handle open. Scope is limited to `app/snapshot.py` and `tests/test_snapshot.py`; use `contextlib.closing` for both paired connections while retaining the existing backup transaction behavior, output validation, atomic publication, and original exceptions. Add a real-handle regression covering both main/relay roles and destination-open/destination-close failures, asserting original exception propagation, source-handle closure, and absence of outputs. RED before production edit, then focused and affected snapshot tests. This repairs connection ownership and does not attribute any prior timing failure.

## PR312 focused regression and fixture qualification

On isolated Python3.13.15 with the 67 pinned CI dependencies (including AnyIO4.15.1 and filelock4.2.0), the closed-loop cleanup control was RED when only the new drain call was removed: the held tracked callback reached the intended early `cancel_http_request` assertion, cleanup did not enter the drain, and the control failed on `drain_entered` in3.10s while its `finally` released and drained the owned worker. The reviewed version was GREEN: the control and the three unchanged original parameter cases passed (4 passed in2.51s). The affected capacity file passed55 tests in37.33s. This establishes the cleanup ordering regression and its fix, but does not show it caused hosted registration timing failures.

The actor/container functional test’s clock control used a real setup stall of0.460s after `session.connection()` returned on the final delivery turn. On the unmodified baseline, setup returned `setup_deadline` and the original delivery assertion failed without emitting a delivery; with only the test-local storage clock copy, the same stall passed while native locks, SQLite, sleeps, global clocks and functional assertions remained unchanged. The focused control set passed18 tests in17.62s, including the eight original parameter cases, deterministic refusal boundaries and native SQLite writer contention. The separate real Relay HTTP contention/identity node passed1 test in1.46s, and the complete affected hooks file passed123 tests in35.05s. These results show the functional fixture no longer consumes the local acquisition clock; they do not establish a 450ms product latency guarantee or a production cause.

The integrated candidate is based on frozen PR312 source `b84a54c156b4fadaa2867409e7ebf6e45c3aef87` and contains only the accepted test cleanup/regression, actor fixture, and this evidence update. Diagnostic-only workflow pins and timing capture are excluded. `git diff --check` passed. The whole-change selector chose `full` for “selection policy, CI, test files, or behavior contracts changed” and reported `python -m pytest tests/ -x -q`. Agent Workflow completed with advisory status after the configured import-linter and Redline steps: the only findings are the existing shadow architecture-review and commit-order advisories; there are no blocking predicates.

The exact Python3.13.15 runtime available locally is the private embeddable distribution whose `_pth` sets isolated mode and whose prior xdist run failed because the child lacked its expected bootstrap marker. It is not a qualified full-suite runtime. Standard CPython3.13.14 is already available through uv; a small child-process probe confirmed that a module present only on process `PYTHONPATH` imports in the child (`isolated=0`, `ignore_environment=0`). That probe does not establish exact patch-version or dependency parity, and no private standard 3.13.14 environment with the incident’s 67 package versions was prepared. No full integrated suite was run. The required full qualification remains open; use an accepted standard-runtime runner or the hosted full matrix rather than repeating the known embedded-runtime harness.
